| `FC05-C10-U086` | **Construction — change-of-rings map for RHom**                                          | §10.7.1 Change of Rings; pp. 398–401; L11465–11469                          | For f:R→S, the universal property gives a natural map f_*RHom_S(A,B)→RHom_R(f_*A,f_*B).                                                                                                                                                | `FC05-C10-U085`, `FC05-C10-U055`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U087` | **Theorem 10.7.6**                                                                       | §10.7.1 Change of Rings; pp. 398–401; L11471–11480                          | For commutative f:R→S, Lf^* is left adjoint to restriction f_* on the stated bounded derived categories; equivalently the derived Hom sets, and more strongly the RHom objects, satisfy the displayed adjunction isomorphism.          | `FC05-C02-U032`, `FC05-C10-U074`, `FC05-C10-U085`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U088` | **Example/comparison — scheme comparison for derived Hom adjunction**                    | §10.7.1 Change of Rings; pp. 398–401; L11497                                | For schemes the analogous RHom adjunction needs a localization argument and a finiteness restriction on the first argument; the source flags this as a limitation of the naive ring proof.                                             | `FC05-C10-U087`                                                    |
| `FC05-C10-U089` | **Example 10.8.1**                                                                       | §10.8 Replacing Spectral Sequences; pp. 402–406; L11515–11521               | For bounded-above complexes over composable rings, the two parenthesizations of a threefold derived tensor product are naturally isomorphic.                                                                                           | `FC05-C10-U071`                                                    |
| `FC05-C10-U090` | **Theorem — Composition Theorem 10.8.2**                                                 | §10.8 Replacing Spectral Sequences; pp. 402–406; L11523–11539               | Given composable triangulated functors G,F and existing right derived functors, there is a canonical natural transformation R(FG)⇒RF∘RG; under the stated good-subcategory hypotheses it is an isomorphism.                            | `FC05-C10-U055`, `FC05-C10-U065`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U091` | **Corollary 10.8.3 (Grothendieck spectral sequences)**                                   | §10.8 Replacing Spectral Sequences; pp. 402–406; L11547–11571               | For left exact G:A→B and F:B→C with enough injectives and the usual acyclicity hypothesis, R^+(FG)≅R^+F∘R^+G and the Grothendieck spectral sequence follows; finite cohomological dimension gives the unbounded version.               | `FC05-C10-U090`, `FC05-C05-U063`, `FC05-C10-U067`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U092` | **Convention/notation — dual composition theorem**                                       | §10.8 Replacing Spectral Sequences; pp. 402–406; L11583                     | Subsequent applications use without further comment the dual isomorphism LF∘LG≅L(FG) when the hypotheses of the Composition Theorem hold.                                                                                              | `FC05-C10-U090`, `FC05-C10-U057`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U093` | **10.8.5 (label type absent in extraction)**                                             | §10.8.1 The Projection Formula; pp. 402–406; L11587–11599                   | For f:R→S, bounded-above right R-complex A, and left S-complex B, the Composition Theorem gives Lf^*A⊗^L_S B≅A⊗^L_R f_*B; after restriction this is the projection formula in D(R).                                                    | `FC05-C10-U074`, `FC05-C10-U090`, `FC05-C10-U071`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U094` | **10.8.6 (label type absent in extraction)**                                             | §10.8.1 The Projection Formula; pp. 402–406; L11603–11609                   | For ring maps R→S→T, derived extension of scalars composes: (Lg^*)(Lf^*)A≅L(gf)^*A; this is the derived-category form underlying the indicated Tor spectral sequence.                                                                  | `FC05-C10-U074`, `FC05-C10-U090`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U095` | **Theorem 10.8.7**                                                                       | §10.8.2 Adjointness of ⊗^L and RHom; pp. 402–406; L11613–11625              | For commutative R and bounded-above B, −⊗^L_R B is left adjoint to RHom_R(B,−) on the stated bounded derived categories; the theorem records the RHom-level isomorphism and its unit and counit.                                       | `FC05-C10-U071`, `FC05-C10-U083`, `FC05-C02-U032`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U096` | **Construction/comparison — change-of-rings RHom composition**                           | §10.8.2 Adjointness of ⊗^L and RHom; pp. 402–406; L11637–11643              | For f:R→S, because Hom_R(S,−) preserves injectives, RHom_S(A,RHom_R(S,B))≅RHom_R(f_*A,B); this is the derived form underlying the base-change-for-Ext spectral sequence.                                                               | `FC05-C02-U033`, `FC05-C10-U083`, `FC05-C10-U090`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U097` | **10.8.8 (label type absent in extraction)**                                             | §10.8.3 Leray Spectral Sequences; pp. 402–406; L11665–11677                 | For f:X→Y, the Leray spectral sequence is promoted to RΓ(X,F)≅RΓ(Y,Rf_*F) on D^+(X), and on unbounded D(X) when the spaces satisfy the stated noetherian finite-Krull-dimension hypotheses.                                            | `FC05-C10-U077`, `FC05-C10-U090`, `FC05-C05-U066`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U098` | **Lemma 10.8.9**                                                                         | §10.8.3 Leray Spectral Sequences; pp. 402–406; L11681–11687                 | For continuous X→Y→Z, direct image sheaves compose strictly: (gf)**F=g**(f_*F).                                                                                                                                                        | `FC05-C05-U066`                                                    |
| `FC05-C10-U099` | **Corollary 10.8.10**                                                                    | §10.8.3 Leray Spectral Sequences; pp. 402–406; L11689–11695                 | The preceding identity derives to R(gf)**F≅Rg**(Rf_*F) on D^+(X), and unboundedly under the stated noetherian finite-dimensional hypotheses.                                                                                           | `FC05-C10-U097`, `FC05-C10-U098`, `FC05-C10-U090`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U100` | **Definition 10.9.1**                                                                    | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11711                 | A spectrum E is a sequence of based spaces E_n with based homeomorphisms E_n≅ΩE_{n+1}; compatible level maps form the category S, with the one-point spectrum as zero object and levelwise products.                                   | —                                                                  |
| `FC05-C10-U101` | **Construction — Looping and Delooping 10.9.2**                                          | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11715                 | Defines ΩE levelwise and Ω^{-1}E by reindexing E_{n+1}; Ω and Ω^{-1} are inverse automorphisms of S, with Ω^{-1} serving as translation in the stable category.                                                                        | `FC05-C10-U100`                                                    |
| `FC05-C10-U102` | **Example 10.9.3 (Sphere spectra)**                                                      | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11717–11723           | Constructs the n-sphere spectrum S^n by stabilization `(S^n)_p=colim_i Ω^i S^{n+p+i}` and records Ω^pS^n=S^{n-p} for all integers n,p.                                                                                                 | `FC05-C10-U101`                                                    |
| `FC05-C10-U103` | **Definition 10.9.4 (The stable category)**                                              | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11725–11731           | Defines π_nE, weak homotopy equivalences of spectra as π_*-isomorphisms, and the stable homotopy/topological derived category D(S)=W̃^{-1}S.                                                                                           | `FC05-C10-U100`, `FC05-C10-U023`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U104` | **Definition 10.9.5**                                                                    | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11737                 | A prespectrum is a sequence D_n with structure maps SD_n→D_{n+1}, equivalently D_n→ΩD_{n+1}; defines functions of prespectra, the category P, the forgetful S→P, and CW prespectra.                                                    | `FC05-C10-U100`                                                    |
| `FC05-C10-U105` | **10.9.6 (label type absent in extraction)**                                             | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11741–11747           | Defines spectrification Ω^∞:P→S on CW prespectra by `(Ω^∞D)_n=colim_i Ω^iD_{n+i}`, with structure maps induced by index shift.                                                                                                         | `FC05-C10-U104`, `FC05-C02-U060`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U106` | **Definition — CW spectrum and S_CW**                                                    | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11749                 | A CW spectrum is a spectrum Ω^∞D for some CW prespectrum D; S_CW is the full subcategory of S on CW spectra.                                                                                                                           | `FC05-C10-U104`, `FC05-C10-U105`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U107` | **Example/comparison — Analogy 10.9.7**                                                  | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11759–11761           | Compares prespectra/spectra with presheaves/sheaves: the source states Ω^∞ is left adjoint to the forgetful functor, paralleling sheafification; it uses this analogy to transport standard constructions from prespectra to spectra.  | `FC05-C10-U105`, `FC05-C02-U058`, `FC05-C02-U064`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U108` | **Example 10.9.8 (Coproduct)**                                                           | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11763                 | The wedge of prespectra is levelwise wedge; applying Ω^∞ gives the wedge/coproduct of spectra.                                                                                                                                         | `FC05-C10-U105`, `FC05-C10-U107`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U109` | **Example 10.9.9 (Suspension)**                                                          | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11765–11769           | Defines suspension SE by spectrifying the levelwise suspended prespectrum and records the natural weak equivalence Ω^{-1}E→SE.                                                                                                         | `FC05-C10-U101`, `FC05-C10-U105`, `FC05-C10-U103`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U110` | **Definition 10.9.10 (Homotopy category)**                                               | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11771–11773           | Defines the cylinder spectrum by spectrification, homotopy of spectrum maps through the cylinder, homotopy classes [E,F], the homotopy category K(S), and its CW full subcategory K(S_CW).                                             | `FC05-C10-U105`, `FC05-C10-U106`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U111` | **Proposition 10.9.11**                                                                  | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11777–11779           | K(S) is an additive category; each [E,F] is an abelian group and composition distributes over addition.                                                                                                                                | `FC05-C10-U110`, `FC05-C01-U010`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U112` | **Corollary 10.9.12**                                                                    | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11781                 | The natural map E∨F→E×F is an isomorphism in K(S).                                                                                                                                                                                     | `FC05-C10-U111`, `FC05-C10-U108`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U113` | **Proposition 10.9.13**                                                                  | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11785–11793           | Every spectrum E admits a natural weak homotopy equivalence C→E from a CW spectrum C; hence K(S_CW) is localizing in K(S).                                                                                                             | `FC05-C10-U103`, `FC05-C10-U106`, `FC05-C10-U110`, `FC05-C10-U037` |
| `FC05-C10-U114` | **Theorem — Whitehead's Theorem 10.9.14**                                                | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11795–11799           | If C is a CW spectrum, every weak equivalence E→F induces [C,E]≅[C,F]; consequently every weak equivalence between CW spectra is a homotopy equivalence.                                                                               | `FC05-C10-U103`, `FC05-C10-U106`, `FC05-C10-U110`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U115` | **Corollary 10.9.15**                                                                    | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11801–11807           | The stable homotopy category D(S) exists and is equivalent to the homotopy category K(S_CW) of CW spectra.                                                                                                                             | `FC05-C10-U103`, `FC05-C10-U113`, `FC05-C10-U114`, `FC05-C10-U039` |
| `FC05-C10-U116` | **Definition/construction — Mapping Cones 10.9.16**                                      | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11811–11817           | For u:E→F, spectrify the levelwise topological mapping cones to define cone(u); the resulting E→F→cone(u)→SE is the Puppe sequence, and a cofibration sequence is any triangle isomorphic to a Puppe sequence.                         | `FC05-C10-U105`, `FC05-C10-U109`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U117` | **Definition — homotopy-commutative diagram**                                            | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11827                 | A diagram of spectra is homotopy commutative when it commutes in K(S).                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC05-C10-U110`                                                    |
| `FC05-C10-U118` | **Proposition 10.9.17**                                                                  | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11829–11843           | Every homotopy-commutative square of spectra can be replaced by a diagram whose lower square commutes strictly, after replacing one spectrum by a mapping-cylinder homotopy equivalent spectrum.                                       | `FC05-C10-U117`, `FC05-C10-U110`                                   |
| `FC05-C10-U119` | **Theorem 10.9.18**                                                                      | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11845                 | K(S_CW), with translation given by suspension/delooping and cofibration sequences as exact triangles, is a triangulated category.                                                                                                      | `FC05-C10-U115`, `FC05-C10-U116`, `FC05-C10-U014`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U120` | **Construction — Geometric Realization 10.9.19**                                         | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11859–11865           | Using Dold–Kan and good truncation, constructs a realization functor Ch(Ab)→S_CW that sends quasi-isomorphisms to weak equivalences and shifts to loopings, hence induces D(Ab)→D(S).                                                  | `FC05-C01-U004`, `FC05-C10-U103`, `FC05-C10-U106`                  |
| `FC05-C10-U121` | **Vista 10.9.20**                                                                        | §10.9 The Topological Derived Category; pp. 407–416; L11867                 | With HZ the realization of Z, the source records the equivalence of D(Ab) with the stable category of HZ-module spectra, carrying derived tensor product to the smash product over HZ.                                                 | `FC05-C10-U120`, `FC05-C10-U071`                                   |

## Appendix A. Category Theory Language

| ID             | Kind / source unit                                                 | Location                                            | Concise data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Earlier FC05 dependencies                                       |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `FC05-CA-U001` | **Definition A.1.1**                                               | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11875                | A category C consists of a class of objects, Hom sets, identity morphisms, and composition functions. The extraction ends with “subject to two axioms:” but the two axioms themselves are absent before A.1.2.                                    | —                                                               |
| `FC05-CA-U002` | **Paradigm A.1.2**                                                 | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11877                | Sets is the paradigm category: objects are sets, morphisms are functions, composition is ordinary composition, and identities are identity functions; the object collection is a proper class rather than a set.                                  | `FC05-CA-U001`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U003` | **Examples A.1.3**                                                 | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11879–11885          | Records Ab, Groups, Rings, left/right module categories, and discrete categories as standard examples; a discrete category has only identity morphisms.                                                                                           | `FC05-CA-U001`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U004` | **Definition/comparison — Small categories A.1.4**                 | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11887–11891          | A category is small when its objects form a set; a poset becomes a small category with at most one arrow p→q, and a one-object category is a monoid, hence a group is a one-object category.                                                      | `FC05-CA-U001`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U005` | **Definition — isomorphism**                                       | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11895                | A morphism f:B→C is an isomorphism when it has a two-sided inverse, which is unique; the paragraph compares categorical isomorphisms with bijections, homeomorphisms, diffeomorphisms, and group elements.                                        | `FC05-CA-U001`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U006` | **A.1.5 (monic, epi, subobject)**                                  | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11897–11899          | Defines monic by left cancellation and epi by right cancellation; in concrete algebraic categories these agree with injective/surjective maps in the stated cases. A subobject is represented by a monic, technically up to mutual factorization. | `FC05-CA-U001`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U007` | **A.1.6 (initial, terminal, zero objects)**                        | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11903                | Defines initial and terminal objects by unique maps out of/into them and a zero object as both; gives Sets and module-category examples.                                                                                                          | `FC05-CA-U001`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U008` | **Definition — zero morphisms, kernels, and cokernels**            | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11905                | In a category with zero object, defines zero maps via B→0→C; defines kernels and cokernels by their universal factorization properties, noting kernels are monic and cokernels epi.                                                               | `FC05-CA-U006`, `FC05-CA-U007`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U009` | **Definition/construction — Opposite Category A.1.7**              | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11909                | C^op has the same objects and reversed morphisms/composition; taking opposites interchanges monic/epi, kernel/cokernel, and initial/terminal objects.                                                                                             | `FC05-CA-U001`, `FC05-CA-U006`, `FC05-CA-U008`                  |
| `FC05-CA-U010` | **Example A.1.8**                                                  | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11911                | For a ring R viewed as a one-object category, R^op reverses multiplication and `(R^op)-mod≅mod-R`; the opposite of R-mod need not itself be S-mod for a ring S.                                                                                   | `FC05-CA-U004`, `FC05-CA-U009`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U011` | **Definition — Products and Coproducts A.1.9**                     | §A.1 Categories; pp. 417–420; L11915–11917          | Defines arbitrary products by projections and their universal property and coproducts dually by injections; records uniqueness up to isomorphism and standard examples in Sets, Groups, and R-mod.                                                | `FC05-CA-U009`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U012` | **Definition — functor**                                           | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11929–11935            | A functor F:C→D maps objects and morphisms, preserves identities and composition, induces maps on Hom sets, and composes in the evident way.                                                                                                      | `FC05-CA-U001`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U013` | **Construction — identity functor and Cat**                        | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11937                  | Defines id_C and forms Cat, the category of small categories and functors, avoiding the set-theoretic difficulty of a category of all categories.                                                                                                 | `FC05-CA-U004`, `FC05-CA-U012`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U014` | **Example/comparison — Hom and Tensor Product A.2.1**              | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11939                  | For a right R-module M, M⊗_R− is a functor R-mod→Ab and Hom_R(M,−) is a functor mod-R→Ab.                                                                                                                                                         | `FC05-CA-U012`, `FC05-C02-U054`                                 |
| `FC05-CA-U015` | **Definition/example — Forgetful Functors A.2.2**                  | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11941                  | A forgetful functor discards specified structure; examples include R-mod→Ab→Sets and their composite.                                                                                                                                             | `FC05-CA-U012`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U016` | **Definition — Faithful Functors A.2.3**                           | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11943                  | F:C→D is faithful when every induced Hom-set map is injective; a category admitting a faithful underlying-set functor U:C→Sets is concrete.                                                                                                       | `FC05-CA-U012`, `FC05-CA-U015`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U017` | **Definition — subcategory**                                       | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11945                  | A subcategory selects objects and morphisms closed under identities and composition; its inclusion functor is faithful.                                                                                                                           | `FC05-CA-U001`, `FC05-CA-U016`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U018` | **Definition — full subcategory**                                  | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11947                  | A subcategory B⊂C is full when Hom_B(B,B′)=Hom_C(B,B′) for all its objects, so it is determined by its object class.                                                                                                                              | `FC05-CA-U017`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U019` | **Definition — full and fully faithful functors**                  | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11949                  | A functor is full when each induced Hom map is surjective and fully faithful when it is both full and faithful; inclusions of full subcategories and the Yoneda embedding are examples.                                                           | `FC05-CA-U016`, `FC05-CA-U018`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U020` | **Definition — Skeletal Subcategories A.2.4**                      | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11951                  | A skeletal subcategory S⊂C is full and contains exactly one representative from each isomorphism class of objects of C.                                                                                                                           | `FC05-CA-U005`, `FC05-CA-U018`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U021` | **Construction — reflection onto a skeletal subcategory**          | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11953                  | Choosing representatives FC∈S and isomorphisms θ_C:C≅FC defines F(f)=θ_C f θ_B^{-1}; this reflection functor is fully faithful.                                                                                                                   | `FC05-CA-U020`, `FC05-CA-U019`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U022` | **Definition/example — Contravariant Functors A.2.5**              | §A.2 Functors; pp. 421–422; L11955–11957            | A contravariant F:C→D is a covariant functor C^op→D, reversing arrows and composition; examples include Hom_R(−,N) and presheaves.                                                                                                                | `FC05-CA-U009`, `FC05-CA-U012`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U023` | **Definition — natural transformation and natural isomorphism**    | §A.3 Natural Transformations; p. 423; L11961–11967  | For F,G:C→D, a natural transformation η:F⇒G assigns η_C:F(C)→G(C) commuting with every morphism square; it is a natural isomorphism when every component is an isomorphism.                                                                       | `FC05-CA-U012`, `FC05-CA-U005`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U024` | **Examples A.3.1**                                                 | §A.3 Natural Transformations; p. 423; L11969–11973  | Gives the torsion-subgroup inclusion, tensor maps induced by a module homomorphism, and precomposition maps on Hom as natural transformations, with links to Tor/Ext and δ-functors.                                                              | `FC05-CA-U023`, `FC05-CA-U014`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U025` | **Definition — Equivalence A.3.2**                                 | §A.3 Natural Transformations; p. 423; L11975        | F:C→D is an equivalence when some G:D→C has natural isomorphisms id_C≅GF and id_D≅FG; skeletal inclusion and forgetting a chosen basis are examples.                                                                                              | `FC05-CA-U023`, `FC05-CA-U020`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U026` | **Definition/construction — Functor Categories A.3.3**             | §A.3 Natural Transformations; p. 423; L11977        | For small I and category A, A^I has functors I→A as objects and natural transformations as morphisms, composed componentwise; if A is abelian then A^I is abelian.                                                                                | `FC05-CA-U013`, `FC05-CA-U023`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U027` | **Example A.3.4 (Yoneda embedding)**                               | §A.3 Natural Transformations; p. 423; L11979        | The Yoneda functor h:I→Sets^{I^op}, i↦Hom_I(−,i), is fully faithful; for an Ab-category it lands in Ab^{I^op}, embedding additive categories as full subcategories of abelian categories.                                                         | `FC05-CA-U019`, `FC05-CA-U022`, `FC05-CA-U026`, `FC05-C01-U062` |
| `FC05-CA-U028` | **A.4.1 (Ab-category)**                                            | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L11985–11993  | An Ab-category has an abelian-group structure on every Hom set with composition bilinear; endomorphisms form rings, and R-mod is the basic example.                                                                                               | `FC05-CA-U001`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U029` | **Definition — additive category**                                 | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L11995        | An additive category is an Ab-category with a zero object and all binary products; finite products then coincide with finite coproducts and are written A⊕B.                                                                                      | `FC05-CA-U028`, `FC05-CA-U007`, `FC05-CA-U011`                  |
| `FC05-CA-U030` | **Definition A.4.2**                                               | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L11997–12003  | An abelian category is additive, every map has kernel and cokernel, every monic is the kernel of its cokernel, and every epi is the cokernel of its kernel.                                                                                       | `FC05-CA-U029`, `FC05-CA-U006`, `FC05-CA-U008`                  |
| `FC05-CA-U031` | **Construction — image factorization in an abelian category**      | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L12014–12020  | Every f:B→C factors as an epi B→im(f) followed by the monic im(f)=ker(coker f)→C; this defines the image subobject.                                                                                                                               | `FC05-CA-U030`, `FC05-CA-U008`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U032` | **Definition — exact sequence**                                    | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L12022        | A sequence A→B→C in an abelian category is exact at B when ker(g)=im(f), which in particular forces gf=0.                                                                                                                                         | `FC05-CA-U030`, `FC05-CA-U031`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U033` | **A.4.3 (AB3 and AB3*)**                                           | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L12024–12027  | AB3 requires all set-indexed coproducts and is called cocompleteness; AB3* requires all set-indexed products and is called completeness.                                                                                                          | `FC05-CA-U011`, `FC05-CA-U030`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U034` | **Example A.4.4**                                                  | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L12029        | Ab and R-mod satisfy AB3 and AB3*; finite abelian groups satisfy neither, torsion abelian groups satisfy AB3 but not AB3*, and countable products/coproducts already suffice for several homological constructions.                               | `FC05-CA-U033`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U035` | **Definition — AB4 and AB4***                                      | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L12033–12034  | AB4 means cocomplete with direct sums of monics monic; AB4* means complete with products of epis epi. This main text resumes after Exercise A.4.4.                                                                                                | `FC05-CA-U033`, `FC05-CA-U006`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U036` | **Example A.4.5**                                                  | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L12036        | Ab and R-mod satisfy AB4 and AB4*; Sheaves(X) is complete but does not satisfy AB4*.                                                                                                                                                              | `FC05-CA-U035`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U037` | **A.4.6 (AB5 and AB5*)**                                           | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L12052–12063  | AB5 means cocomplete with filtered colimits of exact sequences exact, equivalently the stated distributivity identity for directed lattices of subobjects; AB5* is the dual filtered-inverse-limit condition with its dual identity.              | `FC05-CA-U032`, `FC05-CA-U033`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U038` | **Examples A.4.7**                                                 | §A.4 Abelian Categories; pp. 424–426; L12066–12068  | Ab and R-mod satisfy AB5 but not AB5*; Sheaves(X) likewise satisfies AB5 but not AB5*.                                                                                                                                                            | `FC05-CA-U037`, `FC05-C02-U070`                                 |
| `FC05-CA-U039` | **A.5.1 (limit)**                                                  | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12076       | A limit of F:I→A is an object L with compatible maps L→F_i universal among compatible cones; it is unique up to isomorphism, and products are limits over discrete indexing categories.                                                           | `FC05-CA-U012`, `FC05-CA-U011`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U040` | **Definition — complete category**                                 | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12078       | A is complete when every functor F:I→A with small I has a limit; Sets, Ab, and R-mod are complete.                                                                                                                                                | `FC05-CA-U039`, `FC05-CA-U004`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U041` | **Definition — colimit (main text resumes after Exercise A.5.1)**  | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12082       | Dually, a colimit of F:I→A is an object C with compatible maps F_i→C universal among cocones; coproducts are special colimits and colimits in A are limits in A^op.                                                                               | `FC05-CA-U039`, `FC05-CA-U009`, `FC05-CA-U011`                  |
| `FC05-CA-U042` | **Definition — cocomplete category**                               | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12084       | A is cocomplete when every functor from a small indexing category has a colimit; Sets, Ab, and R-mod are cocomplete.                                                                                                                              | `FC05-CA-U041`, `FC05-CA-U004`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U043` | **Construction/comparison — As a Natural Transformation A.5.2**    | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12088       | The diagonal Δ:A→A^I sends A to the constant diagram. The structure maps of a limit form Δ(lim F)⇒F and those of a colimit form F⇒Δ(colim F), anticipating the adjunctions with Δ.                                                                | `FC05-CA-U023`, `FC05-CA-U039`, `FC05-CA-U041`                  |
| `FC05-CA-U044` | **Definition — Filtered Categories and Direct Limits A.5.3**       | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12090–12092 | A small category I is filtered when any two objects admit a common successor and any two parallel arrows become equal after postcomposition; directed posets are the special poset case.                                                          | `FC05-CA-U004`                                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U045` | **Example/comparison — translation category of an abelian monoid** | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12094       | For an abelian monoid M, the category with objects M and morphisms i→j given by multipliers m with mi=j is filtered; this is the translation-category example used for localization.                                                              | `FC05-CA-U044`, `FC05-C10-U027`                                 |
| `FC05-CA-U046` | **Convention/notation — filtered colimits and direct limits**      | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12096       | A filtered colimit is the colimit of a functor from a filtered category; over a directed poset it is also called a direct limit. The paragraph records the colim/lim notation used in the book.                                                   | `FC05-CA-U041`, `FC05-CA-U044`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U047` | **Example A.5.4**                                                  | §A.5 Limits and Colimits; pp. 427–428; L12098       | For I=N, a covariant diagram is a sequence A_0→A_1→⋯ with direct limit colim A_i; a contravariant diagram is an inverse tower with inverse limit.                                                                                                 | `FC05-CA-U044`, `FC05-CA-U046`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U048` | **A.6.1 (adjoint functors)**                                       | §A.6 Adjoint Functors; pp. 429–431; L12102–12114    | L:A→B and R:B→A are adjoint when Hom_B(LA,B)≅Hom_A(A,RB) naturally in A and B; L is the left adjoint and R the right adjoint.                                                                                                                     | `FC05-CA-U012`, `FC05-CA-U023`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U049` | **Example/comparison — standard adjunctions**                      | §A.6 Adjoint Functors; pp. 429–431; L12116–12118    | The free k-vector-space functor is left adjoint to the underlying-set functor; for an R-module B, −⊗_R B is left adjoint to Hom_Ab(B,−) with the indicated module structure.                                                                      | `FC05-CA-U048`, `FC05-CA-U014`, `FC05-CA-U015`                  |
| `FC05-CA-U050` | **Theorem A.6.2**                                                  | §A.6 Adjoint Functors; pp. 429–431; L12122–12132    | An adjunction determines unit η:id_A⇒RL and counit ε:LR⇒id_B; transposition is expressed by R(f)η and εL(g), and the two triangle composites are identities.                                                                                      | `FC05-CA-U048`, `FC05-CA-U023`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U051` | **Definition — Reflective Subcategories A.6.3**                    | §A.6 Adjoint Functors; pp. 429–431; L12142          | A subcategory B⊂A is reflective when its inclusion has a left adjoint L:A→B, called the reflection; for full B the reflected B-objects are naturally isomorphic to themselves.                                                                    | `FC05-CA-U017`, `FC05-CA-U048`                                  |
| `FC05-CA-U052` | **examples of reflective subcategories**                           | §A.6 Adjoint Functors; pp. 429–431; L12144          | Ab is reflective in Groups via abelianization G↦G/[G,G]; sheaves on X are reflective in presheaves via sheafification.                                                                                                                            | `FC05-CA-U051`, `FC05-C02-U058`                                 |
